MSCI 降評風險升高，印尼股市保守看待
2026 年 10 月 27 日
一、 市場動態
1. MSCI 擬將印尼降至獨立市場，降評風險明顯升高：MSCI 於 10 月 15 日宣布，啟動
將印尼股市由「新興市場（Emerging Market）」直接降至「獨立市場（Standalone
Market） 」的諮詢程序，意見徵詢將於2027 年 4 月 30 日截止，最晚於 2027 年 6 月
30 日公布最終決定 。MSCI 原先於今年 1 月提出可能降至 「邊境市場 （Frontier Market
） 」 ，此次則跳過「邊境市場」提出直接將該國股市降至「獨立市場」的方案，顯示對

印尼市場治理的疑慮進一步加深。雖然「邊境市場」仍可能是替代分類方案，但MSCI
強調，除非有明確證據顯示市場治理及運作出現實質改善，否則預期將依目前提案重
新分類，顯示此次降評風險較過去顯著升高。
2. 股權透明度與交易秩序問題為 MSCI 主要疑慮：MSCI 指出，印尼股市長期存在股權
結構不透明、實際自由流通股比例難以確認、疑似協調交易，以及市場監理執法成效
不足等問題，影響國際投資人對市場公平性與可投資性的信心。雖然印尼主管機關已
提出強化持股超過 1%股東資訊揭露、建立高持股集中度（HSC）監理機制，以及規
劃逐步將最低自由流通股比例提高至 15%等改革措施，但MSCI 認為相關改革尚未展
現持續且具實質性的改善成果。由於問題涉及市場制度與治理結構，改善所需時間可
能較一般短期市場波動更長。
3. 股市反彈後再度回落，外資撤離壓力仍高：受降評消息衝擊，印尼雅加達綜合指數 10
月 16 日重挫 11.57%，創 1998 年以來最大單日跌幅，19 日再跌 3.64%，盤中最低
觸及 4,907.37 點 。 雖然20 日至 23 日連續四個交易日反彈 ， 但26 日再度下跌 1.22%

，收於 5,373.09 點，各產業類股全面收黑，顯示市場信心仍不穩定。根據 Reuters
報導，截至 10 月 19 日，2026 年外資已累計淨賣超印尼股票約 48.8 億美元。此外，
印尼證交所 26 日宣布，將引進獨立專家及外國投資人代表參與高持股集中度審查，
並自 11 月起加強調查疑似協調交易，必要時凍結可疑帳戶，以回應 MSCI 對市場制
度的疑慮。
二、 下跌原因分析：
1. 股市：潛在指數剔除與資金外流，持續壓抑市場評價
若印尼股市最終降至「獨立市場」， 原則上將不再納入MSCI 新興市場及邊境市場相關
指數，追蹤相關指數的被動式基金將須依規則調整持股，實際處理方式仍以 MSCI 後
續公告為準。若改降至「邊境市場」，則可能獲得部分邊境市場資金承接，衝擊相對較
小。Reuters 引述多家金融機構估計，印尼股市降至獨立市場可能引發約 30 億至 60

### Page 2

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
億美元被動資金流出，其中 UBS 估計約 40 億美元，並指出主動型基金另有約 150 億
美元潛在撤資風險，惟實際規模仍視最終分類結果及基金策略而定。
2. 匯率：外資撤離增加印尼盾貶值壓力
印尼股市的降評風險不僅衝擊股市，也可能透過外資撤離增加印尼盾貶值壓力。印尼
盾 10 月 16 日盤中一度貶至 18,070 兌 1 美元，為 7 月下旬以來最弱水準之一，當日
收盤約 18,000。若後續國際資金持續流出，可能進一步增加股匯市波動，對以外幣計
價、且未完全避險的印尼基金及 ETF 形成額外壓力。雖然印尼央行可透過匯市干預及

流動性管理措施穩定金融市場，但在資金外流壓力尚未緩解前，印尼盾仍可能維持偏
弱格局。
3. 基金與 ETF：指數權重下滑，單一市場曝險風險升高
MSCI 相關指數調整已對印尼股票產生實質影響。Reuters 引用 LSEG 資料顯示，印尼
在 MSCI 新興市場指數的權重已由 2026 年初的 1.16%降至 9 月底的 0.43%，相關成
分股亦由年初 18 檔減至 9 檔，反映部分指數資金調整壓力已提前出現。對印尼單一
國家基金及 ETF 而言，主要風險來自股市下跌、資金流出及印尼盾波動；主動式基金
雖可透過選股降低部分影響，但仍難以完全迴避整體市場風險。不同產品實際受到的
影響，仍應依追蹤指數、主要持股及匯率避險安排個別評估。
三、 市場看法：
1. 制度性問題難以速解，降評風險將持續牽動市場：我們認為，此次 MSCI 降評風險並
非單純反映經濟成長放緩或企業獲利不佳，而是涉及市場透明度、交易公平性與監理
執法等結構性問題。尤其 MSCI 不僅啟動降評諮詢，更持續限制印尼股票的部分指數

調整，包括不提高外資納入因子、不新增相關成分股，以及不進行向上規模分類調整
，顯示市場可投資性疑慮尚未獲得有效解決。儘管政府試圖推動改革，但制度修正仍
須經過執行與成效驗證，才能重新取得國際投資人的信任，因此短期內降評風險仍難
完全消除。
2. 外資撤離與指數權重下降，市場信心修復恐需較長時間：印尼今年以來外資已大幅賣
超，MSCI 新興市場指數中的權重亦明顯下降，顯示國際投資人對當地市場的疑慮已
逐漸反映於實際資金流向。我們認為，即使印尼最終成功避免降評，部分國際機構投
資人仍可能因市場透明度、流動性及治理品質疑慮而維持較低曝險。此外，MSCI 對
部分指數調整的凍結措施尚未解除，短期內新增指數資金流入機會仍受到限制，預期
國際資金回流與市場評價修復均可能需要較長時間。
3. 監理改革力度加大，但尚不足以扭轉保守展望：印尼政府近期持續加強改革，包括提
高資訊透明度、強化交易監控、對違規交易開罰，以及引進外部獨立人士參與監理審
查。10 月 26 日該國政府進一步提出主動調查疑似協調交易及凍結可疑帳戶等措施，

11 月 2 日起亦規劃將高持股集中度股票排除於雅加達綜合指數之外 。 相關改革有助改

### Page 3

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
善交易秩序及短期市場信心，但部分措施仍待正式實施，且實際成效尚未獲得 MSCI
認可。考量制度性問題改善需要時間，政策利多雖可能帶動短線反彈，但尚不足以改
變對印尼股市中期前景的保守判斷。
4. 股市跌深不代表利空出盡，中期風險報酬吸引力偏低：印尼股市在 10 月 16 日及 19
日大幅修正後，雖曾連續四個交易日反彈，但 26 日再度回落，顯示市場尚未形成穩
定回升趨勢。我們認為，當前股市面臨的不僅是短期賣壓，更包括國際投資人信任下
降及風險溢價提高，即使企業基本面未同步惡化，仍可能因評價下修而承受股價壓力

。因此，短線不排除政策利多或跌深後出現技術性反彈，但在 MSCI 態度、外資流向
與印尼盾走勢明顯改善前，反彈延續性仍有疑慮，印尼股市中期展望維持保守。
四、市場展望：
1. 印尼股市維持保守展望，單一市場風險報酬吸引力不足：考量 MSCI 降評風險尚未解除、
外資撤離壓力持續 ， 以及市場治理改革仍有待驗證 ， 我們認為印尼股市未來數季的風險報
酬吸引力相對有限。即使近期股價明顯回落，仍難認定相關風險已充分反映。相較市場制
度成熟、國際資金流動性較佳的新興市場，印尼股市可能持續面臨較高風險溢價，因此對
單一印尼市場的集中曝險應維持審慎態度。
2. 此事件對新興市場指數與東協指數影響相對有限：根據 MSCI 資料，截至2026 年 9 月底
，印尼股市在 MSCI Emerging Market 指數與 MSCI AC ASEAN 指數中的權重分別約為
0.43%與 7.55%，因此我們認為印尼潛在降評對相關指數的直接衝擊相對有限。此外，新
興市場各國經濟結構與產業組成有所差異 ， 區域型基金可透過跨國分散降低單一市場風險

。惟後續仍須留意印尼事件對整體新興市場投資信心造成的外溢影響 ， 以及全球貿易與匯
率波動風險。

Indicators Update 
2026年 9月消費統計 
サービスを中心に消費全体が弱く、前月から減少

経済調査部 エコノミスト 龐 鈞文

[要約] 
◼ 2026年9月の家計調査における二人以上世帯の実質消費支出は前月比▲2.8％と3カ月
ぶりに減少した。複数の需要側統計を用いて補正した世帯消費動向指数（ CTIミクロ）
で見ても実質消費は同▲1.6％と2カ月連続で減少した。また、供給側統計の1つであ
る商業動態統計では、CPIの財指数で実質化した小売販売額は同▲1.0％だった。総じ
て見れば、9月の個人消費は前月から減少したと判断される。 
◼ 先行きの個人消費は底堅く推移するとみている。名目賃金は最低賃金の引き上げもあ
って上昇が続く見込みである。一方、政府の物価高対策が予定されるものの、原油高
などによる幅広い分野への価格上昇の波及、円安や人件費上昇による価格転嫁などを
通じて個人消費を下押しする可能性には引き続き注意が必要だ。

＜2026年9月の消費総括＞サービスを中心に消費全体が弱い 
需要側統計である家計調査における二人以上世帯の実質消費支出は、前月比▲2.8％と3カ月
ぶりに減少した。複数の需要側統計を用いて補正した世帯消費動向指数（CTIミクロ）で見て
も実質消費は同▲1.6％と 2カ月連続で減少した（図表 1）。また、供給側統計の 1つである商
業動態統計では、CPIの財指数で実質化した小売販売額は同▲1.0％だった。総じて見れば、
2026年 9月の個人消費は前月から減少したと判断される。

＜CTIミクロ・家計調査（需要側）＞全ての費目が減少 
2026年 9月の CTIミクロ（二人以上の世帯）を費目別に見ると、10大費目1のうち、「教養娯
楽」（前月比▲3.3％）、「教育」（同▲6.5％）、「食料」（同▲0.9％）、「光熱・水道」（同▲
3.1％）、「家具・家事用品」（同▲ 4.8％）、「交通・通信」（同▲0.8％）、「保健医療」（同
▲2.4％）、「住居」 （同▲1.6％） 、「被服及び履物」 （同▲3.0％） 、「その他」 （同▲0.4％）の全
ての費目が減少した（図表 2）。全ての費目が減少したのは消費税率が 10％に引き上げられた
2019年 10月以来だ。 
図表 2：実質世帯消費動向指数（CTIミクロ）の前月比

続いて、CTIミクロの10大費目に含まれる個別品目への支出について確認する。「教養娯楽」
は2カ月連続で減少した。教養娯楽用品や教養娯楽耐久財を中心に支出が減少した。「教育」は
3カ月連続で減少した。授業料等を中心に支出が減少した2。「食料」は3カ月ぶりに減少した。
飲料を中心に、穀類などへの支出が減少した。「光熱・水道」は2カ月ぶりに減少した。電気代
を中心に、上下水道料などへの支出が減少した。「家具・家事用品」は2カ月連続で減少した。
家庭用耐久財を中心に、家事雑貨などへの支出が減少した。「交通・通信」は2カ月連続で減少
した。自動車等関係費や通信を中心に支出が減少した。「保健医療」は3カ月連続で減少した。
保健医療用品・器具を中心に支出が減少した。「住居」は2カ月ぶりに減少した。設備修繕・維
持を中心に支出が減少した。「被服及び履物」は2カ月連続で減少した。シャツ・セーター類や
他の被服を中心に支出が減少した。「その他」は2カ月連続で減少した。諸雑費を中心に支出が
減少した。 
消費支出を財・サービス別に見ると（大和総研による季節調整値、図表3中段右）、サービス
（前月比▲3.9％）が2カ月連続で減少し、全体を押し下げた。非耐久財（同▲1.6％）は2カ月
ぶりに、耐久財（同▲3.6％）は 5カ月連続で、半耐久財（同▲3.0％）は 2カ月連続で、いず
れも減少した。 
家計調査における基礎的支出は前月比▲0.6％と2カ月ぶりに減少した。また、選択的支出は
同▲7.6％と 3カ月ぶりに大幅に減少した（いずれも大和総研による季節調整値、図表 3中段
左）。

2 ただし、授業料等は子どもや学生がいる一部の世帯以外はほとんど支出しないため、サンプルサイズが小さ
く振れが大きい。

＜商業動態統計（供給側）＞小売販売額は名目・実質ともに2カ月連続で減少 
2026年 9月の商業動態統計によると、名目小売販売額は前月比▲0.7％と 2カ月連続で減少
した（図表 4、5） 。また、CPIの財指数で実質化した小売販売額は同▲1.0％だった。 
名目小売販売額を業種別に見ると、7業種中 4 業種が前月から減少した。「その他小売業」
（前月比▲2.1％）、「燃料小売業」 （同▲2.7％）ともに 2カ月連続で減少した。「機械器具小売
業」 （同▲2.3％）も 2カ月連続で減少した。家電大型専門店の販売額（大和総研による季節調
整値） （同▲3.2％）などが減少した。「飲食料品小売業」 （同▲0.2％）は 2カ月連続で減少し
た。コンビニのファーストフード及び日配食品（同▲2.3％）と加工食品（同▲1.9％）が減少
した。 
他方、3業種が前月から増加した。「自動車小売業」 （前月比＋0.1％）は 5カ月ぶりにプラス
に転じた。9月の新車販売台数（大和総研による季節調整値）は同＋2.0％と増加しており3、
この結果とも整合的だ。「織物・衣服・身の回り品小売業」 （同＋4.2％）は2カ月ぶりに増加し
た。気温が低下したこと（巻末図表「全国の平均気温・日照時間・降水量」）や休日数が多か
ったことなどにより、秋物商品の販売が好調 だったとみられる。「各種商品小売業」（同＋
2.1％）は 2カ月ぶりに増加した。スーパーの商品販売額（同＋1.0％）と百貨店の商品販売額
（同＋0.5％）が増加した。

図表 4：小売販売額（業種別）の前月比

（注 1）経済産業省による季節調整値。 
（注 2） 「小売業計」は、 「無店舗小売業」を含む。 「その他小売業」は、 「医薬品・化粧品小売業を含むその他
の小売業」 。 
（注 3）シェアは、2025年の数値。 「無店舗小売業」の系列がないため、各系列のシェアを合計しても100％に
はならない。 
（出所）経済産業省統計より大和総研作成

3 詳細は、拙稿「消費データブック（2026/11/5号）」（大和総研レポート、2026年11月 5日）を参照。

7 /9

＜2026年10月の消費＞9月から減少を見込む 
業界統計や個社データ、JCB消費NOWをもとに判断すると、2026年10月の消費は9月から減
少したとみている4。財消費では、10月前半の実績をもとに試算した家電のJCB消費額（大和総
研による季節調整値） 、コンビニのJCB消費額（同） 、新車販売台数（同）とアパレルの売上高
は減少した。一方、百貨店の売上高は堅調に推移した。サービス消費では、宿泊の JCB消費額
（同）は前月から減少した一方、外食のJCB消費額（同）は増加した。

＜先行き＞個人消費は底堅く推移する見込み／価格上昇の広がりに注意 
先行きの個人消費は底堅く推移するとみている。名目賃金の高い伸びが下支えとなるだろう。
ただし、原油高の波及や円安などによる物価上昇が実質賃金を押し下げ、消費の重荷となる可
能性には注意が必要だ。 
名目賃金の伸びは高水準で推移するだろう。厚生労働省によると、全ての都道府県で 10～12
月に最低賃金が引き上げられる。2026年度の最低賃金の全国加重平均額は1,177円で5、引き上
げ率は＋5.0％（前年度は＋6.3％）と高水準を維持した。また、企業収益の改善などを背景に、
高水準の年末賞与が期待される。 
消費者物価について、12月使用分から 2027年 2月使用分までの電気・ガス料金の補助とい
った政府の物価高対策が実施される予定だ6。ただし、原油高などにより、原材料費や輸送費の
上昇を通じて幅広い分野の価格に波及し、円安や人件費の高騰などによる価格転嫁も物価を押
し上げ、消費を下押しする可能性には引き続き注意が必要だ。また、帝国データバンクによる
と、飲食料品の 9～10月の大規模な値上げラッシュは一服したが、11月の値上げ品目数は前年
11月の約 10倍規模となり、引き続き高水準で推移している。賃上げや物流費、エネルギーコ
ストの上昇など、食品を取り巻くコスト環境が楽観視できる状況にはないため、値上げは続く
とみている7。 
また、金利上昇による住宅ローン等の利払い負担増が個人消費にもたらす悪影響にも警戒す
る必要がある8。さらに、消費動向調査（内閣府）によると、10月の消費者態度指数（二人以
上の世帯、季節調整値）は 35.0（前月差▲0.4pt）と 2カ月連続で小幅に低下し、依然として
低水準にあり（巻末図表「消費者マインド」参照） 、節約志向の高まりに引き続き注意が必要
だ。

4 詳細は、拙稿「消費データブック（2026/11/5号）」（大和総研レポート、2026年11月 5日）を参照。 
5 厚生労働省「全ての都道府県で地域別最低賃金の改定額が答申されました ～答申での全国加重平均額は昨
年度から 56円引上げの1,177円～」（2026年9月3日） 
6 資源エネルギー庁「電気・ガス料金支援」（2026年11月9日閲覧） 
7 帝国データバンク「『食品主要 195社』価格改定動向調査 ― 2026年 11月 飲食料品値上げ、年間 2万品
目超え 11月は 1477品目 秋の『値上げラッシュ』は一服」（2026年10月 30日） 
8 詳細は、神田慶司・畑中宏仁・中村華奈子・横田凱「日本経済見通し：2026年8月」（大和総研レポート、 
2026年8月24日）を参照。

研 究  報 告                
義隆(2458.TT) 
產業：電子–半導體 
深受 NB 出貨趨緩影響，短期營運受壓抑 
 投資評價與建議 
NB客⼾拉貨⼒道趨弱將壓抑整體營運動能 ， 無⼈機相關產品為主要成⻑來源 ，
2027年營運展望保守，給予30倍PER*2027年EPS評價。 
 重點評論分析 
4Q26 NB產業承壓，營運同步面臨逆風：NB客⼾拉貨保守，雖有短/急單，但
延續性恐不佳 ， 僅有無⼈機相關產品表現較佳 ， 推估營收16億元 ， QoQ-27.9% ，
且仍持續面臨供應鏈成本壓⼒，推估毛利率43.9%，EPS 0.51元。 
短期NB缺料問題難解，2027年營運成⻑動能恐受限：展望2027年，IDC預估
PC出貨量YoY-4.1% ， 而義隆營收約80%以上與NB相關 ， 因此短期公司營運亦
同處於逆風，主要動能來自非NB產品的無⼈機：公司與中研院合作，利用自身
演算法優勢，推出Mini LED local dimming(區域調光)與ISP產品，1H26出貨
以ISP為主，ASP約5~6美元，7月已開始出貨AI Box，ASP約300~500美元，
已通過雷虎測試並開始出貨，2H26 ASP明顯提升，為2026年最佳成⻑動能，
也是推升AI應用占比提升主要動能，2026年AI營收YoY+40%，也是未來公司
重心，且公司具備MIT優勢，將積極爭取國防訂單，未來具成⻑潛⼒。整體而
言，短期NB客⼾拉貨恐保守，主要仰賴無⼈機等非NB產品推升，營運成⻑較
受限。 
 投資風險 
NB需求進⼀步下滑或非NB貢獻不如預期。

### Page 2

2

| 營運概況  
3Q26營運受NB影響而趨緩，獲利低於預期：3Q26營收22.2億元，QoQ-30.8%，主因1H26 NB客⼾提前拉貨及
缺料造成拉貨⼒道下滑，毛利率44.7%，主因產品組合轉差及供應鏈成本提升(包含模組的被動元件及PCB漲價)，
費用因嚴控成本而下滑至6.96億元，業外主要來自金融評價損失，EPS 0.58元，優於預期。 
4Q26 NB產業承壓，營運同步面臨逆風：NB客⼾面臨CPU、記憶體缺料問題，拉貨保守，雖有短/急單，但延續
性恐不佳，僅有無⼈機相關產品表現較佳，推估營收16億元，QoQ-27.9%，且仍持續面臨供應鏈成本壓⼒，推估
毛利率43.9%，EPS 0.51元。 
短期NB缺料問題難解 ， 2027年營運成⻑動能恐受限 ：受記憶體與CPU缺貨持續壓抑NB出貨成⻑性 ， 以及NB漲價 、
AI排擠商用NB需求等因素，雖1H26提前拉貨推升公司營運仍佳，然自3Q26起NB客⼾需求趨緩，使得公司營運逐
季下滑，展望2027年，IDC預估PC出貨量YoY-4.1%，而義隆營收約80%以上與NB相關，因此短期公司營運亦同
處於逆風，主要動能來自1)Haptic滲透率持續提升，因Haptic ASP達15美元，明顯優於傳統clickpad 2美元，將
帶動觸控板ASP提升，2026年Haptic營收貢獻約3.5~4億元(2025年約0.6億元)，2027年也將持續提升；2)ADAS
來自電動巴士，2026年出貨double，已有第六位客⼾開始小量出貨，2027年持續成⻑，主要來自政府電動巴士
補助所推升，政府最近也補助電動物流車，2027年確實落地後，公司將有電動物流車的成⻑機會，量也比電動巴
士多很多；3)無⼈機：公司與中研院合作，利用自身演算法優勢，推出Mini LED local dimming(區域調光)與ISP
產品，1H26出貨以ISP為主，ASP約5~6美元，7月已開始出貨AI Box，ASP約300~500美元，已通過雷虎測試並
開始出貨，目前僅作前端光學模組，2026年底將推出避障功能，四鏡頭方案約500美元，十二鏡頭方案將達1,000
美元 ， 2H26 ASP明顯提升 ， 為2026年最佳成⻑動能 ， 也是推升AI應用占比提升主要動能 ， 2026年AI營收YoY+40% ，
也是未來公司重心，且公司具備MIT優勢，將積極爭取國防訂單，未來具成⻑潛⼒。整體而言，短期NB客⼾拉貨
恐保守，主要仰賴無⼈機等非NB產品推升，營運成⻑較受限。 
2027年營運動能受限，維持中立評等：NB客⼾拉貨⼒道趨弱將壓抑整體營運動能，無⼈機相關產品為主要成⻑來
源，2027年營運展望保守，維持中立評等，給予30倍PER*2027年EPS評價。

### Page 1

기아 (000270)

EARNINGS PREVIEW Company Analysis | 자동차/로봇 | 2026.10.15  
잘 싸웠지만 아쉬운 3분기

3Q26 Preview 
기아는 3Q26E 매출액 31.1조원(+8.2% YoY, -6.0% QoQ), 영업이익 2.2조원
(+53.1% YoY, -14.9% QoQ, OPM 7.2%)을 기록, 컨센서스를 하회할 전망이다. 
물량은 좋았으나, 아쉬운 믹스와 환율 
글로벌 도매 판매 84.7만대(+7.8% 이하 YoY), 중국 제외 판매량은 83.0만대
(+8.6%)를 기록했다. EV·HEV 확대에 따른 ASP 상승은 지속되고 있다. 전쟁으로 인
한 원자재 상승으로 3분기 재료비 부담은 전분기 대비 3,000억~4,000억원 증가할 
것으로 예상된다. EV 판매 비중은 2분기 13%에서 3분기 15%, 유럽은 35%에서 
43%로 확대되며 유럽 내 마진 희석은 불가피하다. 원화 강세에 따른 영업상 부담은 
판매보증충당부채 환입 등으로 상쇄돼 3분기 환율 영향은 제한적일 전망이다. 판매보
증충당부채 환입은 QoQ +5,000억원 수준으로 추정된다. 4분기부터 환율 효과는 부정
적일 것이며, 환율 10원 변동 시 연간 손익 민감도는 약 1,500억원 수준이다. 
투자의견은 Buy를 유지하고 목표주가는 165,000원으로 하향 
미국 조지아 공장의 텔루라이드 HEV(2.8만대)와 HMGMA의 스포티지 HEV(1.0만대) 
신규 생산으로 북미의 견조한 HEV 수요에 대응하며, 2027년 믹스 개선이 기대된다. 
유럽은 EV3·EV5로 점유율을 확대하는 가운데 유럽 내 할인율 하락과 EV 잔존가치 
회복으로 판매 여건이 개선되고 있다. 향후 IAA 규제에 따른 법인·플릿 혜택 제한은 
중국 업체의 원가 우위를 줄일 수 있을 전망이다. 
원화 약세 지속을 반영해 이익 추정치를 하향하며, 이에 따라 목표주가를 하향한다.  
다만, 현재 주가는 12M Fwd P/E 기준 4.8배로 Legacy OEM 평균(7.7배)보다 낮은 
수준, 저가 매수 기회라 판단된다. 현재 배당 수익률은 6% 수준으로, 상대적으로 높은 
배당 매력은 주가 하방을 지지할 전망이다. 
 Analyst 이병근 
bglee@ls-sec.co.kr 
Buy (유지)

목표주가 (하향) 165,000 원 
현재주가 108,600 원 
상승여력 51.9 %

Document 321: Cliff Notes_ whiplash (2026-11-12)

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: whiplash
By Ryan Wells & Elliot Clarke
11:20 November 06 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
In Australia, the RBA delivered another 25bp rate increase at its November meeting, taking the cash
rate to 4.85%. The move was widely expected, reflecting the Board’s continued concern around
inflation following the recent run higher in global energy prices, firmer domestic capacity pressures and

signs that inflation expectations have edged up. Inflation is now not expected to return to the mid-point
of the target band until mid-2028. This is despite weaker growth expectations and the unemployment

### Page 2

rate trending up to 5.1% by end-2028 on the RBA’s forecasts.
Importantly, though, the decision was not unanimous, with three members preferring to remain on hold
given downside risks to activity and the restraining influence of slower growth. Governor Bullock’s
suggestion that it was time to “take stock” after five rate increases also struck a less hawkish note,
while the statement dropped September’s explicit reference to raising rates further “if needed”. In our

view, February represents the next plausible opportunity for another hike, but we see this only as a risk.
Policy’s effect on housing is becoming increasingly visible. Cotality home prices fell a further 1.2% in
October to now be 6.7% below March’s peak. The cumulative price decline is approaching the scale of
previous housing corrections but appears to have regained momentum after September’s rate
increase. Dwelling approvals saw a 5.1% headline gain in September, but this was driven by volatile

unit approvals while detached house approvals fell sharply, suggesting higher rates and the
established-market downturn are beginning to weigh on the construction pipeline.
Official data on nominal household spending meanwhile rose 0.6% in September and 2.1% for Q3;
however, much of the recent acceleration looks to be price-driven, particularly for fuel. Real spending
volumes expanded by a much more modest (but still solid) 0.7% in Q3.
The Westpac-DataX Consumer Panel provides a granular look at income, spending and saving flows.

The latest update suggests that, beneath the resilient headline story, households are increasingly being
squeezed, particularly those with low savings buffers. With another rate increase now delivered,
housing wealth declining and sentiment weak, Q3 increasingly looks like the high-water mark for
growth in consumer spending.
Lastly for Australia, in September the goods trade surplus widened sharply to $2.8bn, with non-
monetary gold trade yet again adding considerable noise month-to-month. The most notable

development was another exceptionally strong result for data-processing equipment imports, taking the
Q3 total to $11.1bn. This is another clear marker of the scale of Australia’s data-centre investment
boom, which should feature prominently in both business investment and imports in the Q3 National
Accounts.
Offshore, the world has been focused on the US mid-term elections. In the House, Senate and a series
of State Governor races, the Democrats experienced resounding victory. Come 2027, the Democrats

will have a comfortable majority in both houses of Congress and are expected to make use of this
strength to respond to voter concerns, particularly over cost-of-living pressures. The election result also
limits President Trump’s ability to fund an extended military action against Iran. Notably, both
immediately before and after the election, President Trump and the administration have exhibited a
greater interest in furthering negotiations with Iran. It is therefore not a surprise that Brent oil has

backed off its most recent highs around USD118 last Friday, although the scale of the drop to near
USD93 yesterday is arguably too optimistic. Brent has since traded back up to above USD99, still 16%
below Friday’s peak.
While the initial reaction to the US election result was a robust broad-based rally in major equity
indexes, concerns over the potential for tighter regulation of AI and the related infrastructure buildout

### Page 3

Related articles
jolted US equities lower overnight – the Nasdaq falling 5.5% and S&P500 4.4%, both a multiple of the
prior session’s gains. That said, US term interest rates and FOMC rate expectations have also been
scaled back; so, while tech may come under greater scrutiny, a foundation for better outcomes across
the rest of the US economy may become evident in time. US data out this week continued to suggest
their economy is in robust health overall. And, if tensions in the Middle East abate, the FOMC’s

concerns over US inflation should subside. Gaining enough comfort for the FOMC to not hike in
December is a high bar, but the probability of further hikes in 2027 should continue to fall.
Turning then to the UK, the Bank of England raised Bank Rate by 25bps to 4.00% at their November
meeting as expected. On the Bank's baseline expectation, UK inflation is forecast to rise from 3.5%yr
at September to 5.00% by mid-2027. Activity data has also surprised to the upside in recent months,

though "there remains limited evidence of knock-on effects on other prices and wages".
In the press conference, Governor Bailey recognised the nascent improvement in energy price
pressures over the past 48 hours and the effectiveness of monetary policy but also conveyed that
"Unless progress towards resolving the conflict is made, or there is other news which softens the risk to
inflationary pressures, a further increase in Bank Rate may be warranted". Governor Bailey's tone and

comments made in the minutes by other Committee members did not suggest this was a decision that
needs to be made in December, but rather a risk to be judged as additional information is received.
This points to further tightening being more likely in early 2027 than before, and only if conditions / risks
warrant.

Document 332: CPI：10月环比双超预期，核心通胀连续三月黏在0.3%

Source PDF: all report pdf/CPI：10月环比双超预期，核心通胀连续三月黏在0.3%.pdf
Report source: 浙商期货
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 6

### Page 1

CPI ： 10 月环比双超预期，核心通胀连续三月黏在 0.3%
期货研究报告
发布时间： 2026 年 11 月 10 日
适用周期： 2026 年 11 月 10 日 —2026 年 12 月 10 日
浙商期货有限公司　期货交易咨询业务资格：证监许可 [2011]1287 号
【撰写人】浙商期货有限公司  郑弘 (Z0015227)
【咨询电话】 0571-87215357
核心结论： 10 月 CPI 环比 +0.4% 、核心 CPI 环比 +0.3% ，双双高于预期，核心通胀连续三个月停在 0.3% 附近，未给出降温证据。住房环比 +0.3% 贡献整体涨幅三成以上，食品
由持平转为 +0.5% ，能源涨幅由 9 月的 +5.2% 收窄至 +0.7% ，是唯一明确的缓和项。由于 2025 年 10 月政府停摆导致基期数据缺失， BLS 本月未公布同比，市场定价完全转向
环比读数。数据不支持 12 月暂停加息， 25bp 为基准情形、 50bp 进入视野；同比口径要等 12 月 10 日才能恢复。

看点一｜总量环比双超预期： CPI 环比 +0.4% （预期 +0.3% ，前值 +0.6% ），核心 CPI 环比 +0.3% （预期 +0.2% ，前值 +0.3% ），季调 CPI 指数由 336.30 升至 337.48 。
看点二｜主矛盾回到住房与食品：住房环比 +0.3% 、占月度涨幅三成以上，业主等价租金 +0.3% 、租金 +0.2% ；食品由持平转为 +0.5% ，杂货与外出就餐同涨。
看点三｜能源是唯一缓和项：能源环比 +0.7% （ 9 月 +5.2% ），汽油 +1.0% （ 9 月 +9.1% ），电费 +0.9% ，天然气价格下跌。
看点四｜政策性含义清晰：这是 12 月 8-9 日议息会议前最后一份 CPI ，市场已完全消化加息 25bp 并加大对 50bp 的押注。
一、总体数据一览

注： 2026-10 仅有环比，同比因基期缺失未公布；同比列取投研数据库最新可得月（ 2026-09 、 2026-08 ）用于对照。环比为  BLS 口径。
10 月同比缺位是本月最重要的技术事实： BLS 明确表示， 2025 年 10 月政府停摆期间未能采集价格数据且无法追溯补采，基期缺失导致 12 个月同比无法计算，实际收入同比同样不发布。因此本月不能沿
用 “ 同比为主线 ” 的常规框架，环比与三个月年化成为唯一可比的定价依据。
二、分项深度拆解
2.1 能源：涨幅大幅收窄，但未转为负贡献

能源环比由 9 月的 +5.2% 降至 +0.7% ，其中汽油由 +9.1% 降至 +1.0% ，电费 +0.9% ，天然气价格下跌形成部分抵消。同比端能源仍在 20.4% 的高位（ 9 月，数据库口径），对整体 CPI 的推升未消退，只是边

际减弱。结论是能源从 “ 加速器 ” 降为 “ 稳定器 ” ，尚不构成下拉力量。
环比来源： BLS 官方数据表（经智通财经、金十数据转述一致）；同比来源：浙商投研  HGEOV210103 （能源当月同比）， 2026-07 至 2026-09 。
2.2 核心商品：二手车转为拖累，商品端压力有限

核心商品端仍无系统性压力：信息技术硬件与服务同比连续两月 -1.1% ，服装同比由 3.9% 回落至 3.4% ，两者均在回落通道。 10 月环比层面，二手车价格转为下跌 1.2% 、通信服务成本下降，是核心 CPI
没有更超预期的主要缓冲；关税对商品端的第二轮传导尚未在读数中显现。

2.3 核心服务：住房重新成为第一推手

住房环比 +0.3% ，对月度整体涨幅贡献三成以上，业主等价租金 +0.3% 、租金 +0.2% ，住房黏性未破。运输服务项分化最明显：交通运输服务环比 +0.8% 、机动车保险 +0.7% 、机票 +0.4% ，同比已升至
7.9% （ 9 月）；娱乐与医疗保健价格环比上涨但幅度温和，医疗保健同比降至 1.5% ，是核心服务里唯一持续走低的板块。核心服务整体呈现 “ 住房稳、交通强、医疗弱 ” 的格局。

四、市场反应与货币政策
📊  利率预期定价：数据公布前，利率期货已高度定价 12 月加息；公布后，市场完全消化 12 月加息 25 个基点，并明显加大对加息 50 个基点的押注，同时对 2027 年年中前至少再加息两次的定价上升。数
据走强而非走弱，是定价方向没有再摇摆的直接原因。按公司数据著录规则，此处概率仅作方向表述，不引用具体数值。
🧭  官员表态：理事沃勒在数据公布前已明确支持 12 月加息，并设置了一个可验证的门槛 —— 若核心 CPI 环比在 0.3% 或更高，应考虑加息 50 个基点；若通胀改善，则支持 25 个基点。 10 月核心 CPI 环比

+0.3% 恰好落在其 50bp 门槛上，这是本月数据对政策路径最直接的影响。
关键错位：数据公布前，市场共识曾预期核心 CPI 环比仅 +0.2% （部分机构更低），主要依据是健康保险统计方法重置与二手车价格回落等 “ 软因素 ” 。实际 +0.3% 说明这些技术性下拉未能压过住
房与食品的推动。这也意味着，即使后续出现单月走软，只要住房不降，核心通胀的粘性判断难以改变。
五、展望与风险
5.1 情景推演
通胀温和回落
40%
11 月环比回到 0.2% 附近，住房与运输服务同步降温，核心通胀折年率
向 2% 靠拢。
触发条件：住房环比降至 0.2% 以下、能源转负、油价维持 100 美元下方。
能源反复
35%
中东谈判反复推升油价，能源重新成为月度涨幅主力，核心商品跟随
成本端抬升。
触发条件：霍尔木兹通航未实质恢复、布伦特重回 110 美元上方。
滞胀风险
25%
通胀连续三月 0.3% 叠加消费放缓，政策被迫更紧，增长与通胀反向恶
化。
触发条件：核心 CPI 连续第四月 ≥0.3% 、零售与消费者信心同步走弱。

### Page 6

当前最核心的不确定因素：能源向核心的二次传导。能源价格同比仍在 20% 以上，对燃料敏感行业已开始转嫁成本；若传导在四季度扩散，核心服务与核心商品的降温节奏会被推后， 50bp 加息的概率随
之上升。另一个不可忽视的变量是统计口径本身 —— 同比在 12 月 10 日恢复后，市场会重新得到一个 “ 可读的 ” 通胀水平，届时的重定价可能比单月环比更剧烈。
5.2 关键风险矩阵
🔴  油价反弹风险
中东局势若再度升级，能源将从稳定器回到推手，能源同比在 20% 以上的基数上继续抬升，直接压低核心
通胀回落的可信度。
🔴  关税第二轮冲击
商品端目前尚有二手车与通信服务对冲，一旦关税成本在服装、家电等品类集中体现，核心商品将转为正
贡献，核心 CPI 难以下破 0.3% 。
六、结论
1. 总量判断：10 月 CPI 环比 +0.4% 、核心环比 +0.3% ，双双高于预期，价格压力在中期选举前仍在累积，未出现实质性降温证据。
2. 能源主因转换：能源环比由 +5.2% 收窄至 +0.7% ，是本月唯一明确的缓和项；但同比仍在 20.4% 高位，仅为边际减弱，不构成下拉。

3. 核心通胀分化：住房环比 +0.3% 贡献整体涨幅三成以上、交通运输服务与车险走强，医疗保健同比降至 1.5% 、信息技术与服装同比回落，核心服务强、核心商品弱。
4. 政策路径：市场完全消化 12 月加息 25bp 并加大 50bp 押注，核心 CPI 连续三月 0.3% 恰好触及沃勒提出的 50bp 门槛； 25bp 为基准情形， 50bp 为上行风险。
5. 不确定性警示：本月无同比读数，单月环比不足以宣告趋势；真正的验证点在 12 月 10 日同比恢复后的 11 月数据，以及能源向核心的传导是否扩散。
数据说明：① 10 月 CPI 与核心 CPI 的 12 个月同比未公布，原因为 2025 年 10 月政府停摆期间 BLS 未采集价格数据且无法追溯补采，基期缺失；实际收入同比同样未公布。② 10 月环比与分项环比取自 BLS 官方数据表，投研数据库尚未更新 10 月
值。③同比历史序列取自浙商投研数据库，最新更新至 2026-09 。④利率期货概率为媒体引用值，按公司数据著录规则仅作方向表述。⑤报告中不含债券收益率、汇率、贵金属等资产价格明细。

务必向专业人士咨询并谨慎决策。

動態更新  運動用品  ‧美國

Under Armour 
(UAA.O/UAA US)

客流量下滑考驗 
重要訊息  
Under Armour  2Q FY27 (7-9 月；財年止於 3 月) 營運約當 損平。儘管 FY27
營收年減中個位數之 展望不變，管理層下調毛利率指引，反映考量油價、
運價和庫存清倉等衝擊迎面而來 。 
評論及分析  
2Q FY27 營運保持損平 。2Q FY27 每股盈餘 0.00 美元，符合預期。營收
12.4 億美元，年減幅度擴大至 7%，反映全產品 /通路需求 皆轉差。地區別
方面，北美、歐非中東及亞太平洋銷售分別年減 7%、9%、9%，僅拉美
為唯一成長市場 ，年增 3%。再加上通 路、地區組合不利與折扣力道加大，
毛利率延續衰退走勢， 年減 0.3、季減 7.1 個百分點至 47.0%，拖累營利率
至僅 1.2%，年減 0.1、季減 3.1 個百分點。  
產業困境 。管理層觀察到歐美客流量下滑與同業促銷力道加大風險，預計
3Q FY27 營收將年減 8-9% (連三季惡化 )，劣於市場共識之 4%衰退；其中，
北美及歐非中東將為 風暴中心， 分別年減低雙位數、高個位數，亞太則 將
改善至約年持平。加上油價和運價上漲、庫存清倉與匯率不利 等因素疊加
影響，FY27 毛利率指引下調至 47.2-47.2% (前次為 47.7-48.2%)，且年增
1.7-2.2 個百分點中約有 1.5 個百分點係來自美國退稅挹注 。 
庫存去化策略 。2Q FY27 庫存週轉天數年增 7%、但季減 17%至 149 天，
仍高於過去十年同期平均水準之 131 天，主因營運規模縮減凸顯在途存貨
較高。管理層表示 正與合作工廠協商推遲部分發貨，同時考慮 2H FY27 從
批發端去化庫存，但將避開同業清倉甩賣的場所。 公司計畫持續減少產品
SKU、聚焦高端或細分領域 (例如美式足球釘鞋已成為美國第二大品牌 )，
同時簡化工作流程 ，確保長期利潤率改善空間 。 
投資建議  
考量歐美客流量逆風 與促銷影響 仍延續，我們維持 Under Armour 「持有」
評等，目標價改採 FY28 每股淨值 1.6 倍推算，調整為 5.5 美元。 
投資風險  
上檔風險為消費較預期強勁與品牌 重拾人氣。

Under Armour 11 月 6 日法說會重點摘要  
 2Q FY27 (財年止於 3 月；7-9 月) 每股盈餘為 0.00 美元，符合預期。
營收 12.4 億美元，年減幅度擴大至 7%，反映全產品及全通路需求皆
轉差。受通路、地區組合不利與 折扣力道加大 影響，毛利率年減 0.3、
季減 7.1 個百分點至 47.0%，拖累營利率至僅 1.2%。 
 產品別營收：服飾  (佔總營收 71%)、鞋類 (19%) 及配件  (8%) 全面
惡化至年減 6%、11%、7%。 
 市場別營收：北美 (佔總營收 59%)、歐非中東  (23%) 及亞太平洋  
(13%) 分別年減 7%、9%、9%，符合公司指引。拉美  (4%) 年增
3%，為唯一成長市場。 
 通路別營收：批發業務  (佔總營收 58%) 年減 6%，再度轉弱，主因
全價商品銷售疲軟及部分分銷管道遇 逆風。直接面對消費者  (DTC，
40%) 衰退擴大至 8%，反映客流量壓力導致 直營店與電商業務分別
年減 7%、12%。 
 2Q FY27 庫存週轉天數年增 7%、但季減 17%至 149 天，仍高於過去
十年同期平均水準之 131 天，主因在途存貨較高 。公司正透過與合作
工廠協商推遲部分發貨， 同時考慮 2H FY27 從批發端去化庫存 ，但將
避開同業清倉甩賣 的場所。  
 FY27 營收維持年減中個位數之 展望，但基於油價和運價上漲、庫存
清倉與匯率不利等因素影響， 將毛利率指引下調至 47.2-47.2% (前次
為 47.7-48.2%)，且年增 1.7-2.2 個百分點中 約有 1.5 個百分點係 來自
美國退稅挹注。加上組織轉型與重組費用認列，預計每股虧損 0.02
至每股盈餘 0.02 美元。 
 短期而言，管理層觀察到歐美客流量下滑與同業促銷力道加大風險，
預計 3Q FY27 營收將年減 8-9%；其中，北美及歐非中東將分別年減
低雙位數、高個位數，亞太約呈年持平。 加上毛利率年增 0.3-0.5 個
百分點展望， 3Q FY27 營運落後市場預期。

圖 6: 公司概況  圖 7: 服飾貢獻營收最大比重 
成立於 1996 年， Under Armour 為國際領導運動品牌，致力
提供專業運動服飾、鞋類及配件；其產品採用創新材料技術，
包括吸濕排汗、溫度控管、緩衝與布料永續利用等，目的在
創造差異化，並提升消費者品牌 體驗。美洲為 FY26 營收主要
來源地區，佔比達 58%，其次分別為歐非中東 24%、亞太平
洋 14%、拉丁美洲 4%。

股價說明  等級 定義 
 增加持股  (OP) 對個股持正面看法，預期個股未來十二個月的表現超越凱基證券集團所追蹤的相關市場的 總
報酬。  
 持有  (N) 對個股持中性看法，預期個股未來十二個月的表現符合凱基證券集團所追蹤的相關市場的總
報酬。 
 降低持股  (U) 對個股持負面看法，預期個股未來十二個月的表現低於凱基證券集團所追蹤的相關市場的總
報酬。 
 未評等 (NR) 凱基證券未對該個股加以評等。  
 受法規限制  
未評等 (R) 
受凱基證券集團內部政策和 /或相關法令限制使凱基證券集團無法進行某些形式的資訊交
流，其中包括提供評等給投資人參考。  
  *總報酬 = (十二個月目標價 -現價)/現價

Document 336: Downgrade of global equities to a Core allocation

Source PDF: all report pdf/Downgrade of global equities to a Core allocation.pdf
Report source: 渣打銀行 (Market Watch)
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 9
Status: ok

### Page 1

Downgrade of global 
equities to a Core allocation  
Summary 
Event: We are downgrading global equities from Overweight 
to a Neutral/Core allocation. We continue to maintain a 
Neutral allocation to fixed income, Overweight on Gold and 
Underweight on Cash. There are more details in our Global 
Market Outlook publication on 30 October 2026. 
What does the equity downgrade mean?  
This downgrade implies a slightly lower allocation to global 
equities within a diversified portfolio. For our CIO Foundation 
Balanced portfolio, this would imply an equity allocation of 
52.5%, down from 53 .2%. We continue to expect positive 
returns for global equities  in the coming 6 -12 months , 
though equity investors may need to recalibrate expectations 
lower, compared with the 15 -20% annualised gains 
experienced in the last few years. Our capital market 
assumptions still suggest a mid -to-high single digit annual 
returns over the next few years, consistent with a transition 
to a mid -cycle bull market . Consensus estimates point to 
16% earnings growth for global equities in 2027, which 
should overcome various headwinds to deliver positive 
returns for equities over the next 12 months. Investors should 
thus, continue to maintain exposure to equities  within a 
diversified portfolio. 
In addition, we see a changing playbook  to prepare for 
higher volatility, more active management and greater 
diversification, as discussed below. 
What does it mean for regional equities 
positioning? 
Within a global equities portfolio, we remain Overweight US 
and Asia ex -Japan (AxJ) equities , which are expected to 
deliver the highest earnings growth in 2027 amongst the 
major regions. This growth is led by the technology sector, 
which carries the largest weight in both regions. Within the 
diversified equities portfolio, we maintain Core allocations to

Euro area equities, which offer lower valuations, and Japan 
equities, which is backed by a reflating economy. We are 
Underweight UK equities, where the sector composition 
offers lower growth prospects in 2027. Within the AxJ region, 
we are Overweight Taiwan equities, with attractive structural 
growth driven by its semiconductor industry. We are also 
Overweight India equities, which offers diversified non-tech 
growth drivers. Taiwan and India equities both generate 
attractive returns on equity. We maintain Core allocations to 
China equities, with low valuations, and Korea equities, with 
explosive earnings growth in its semiconductors industry, but 
volatile price movements. We are Underweight ASEAN, 
which offers relatively lower earnings growth. 
What are the implications for equity sectors? 
We are Overweight these sectors globally: US 
information technology, US healthcare, US financials, US 
materials, Europe ex -UK financials, and Japan 
financials. 
A framework we use is to break the market into four key 
sector building blocks. Tech is the largest block, 
encompassing the Information Technology, Communication 
Services and Consumer Discretionary sectors. Put 
differently, the segment spans five major industries: 
semiconductors, hardware, internet, software, and IT 
services. Within the US market, we expect this segment to 
deliver c.30% earnings growth in 2027, extending the strong 
momentum seen in 2026. 
Financials and Real Estate  are the next building block, 
comprising these two sectors. We expect this segment to 
continue delivering low -teens earnings growth in 2027, 
supported by resilient economic activity and healthy credit 
trends. 
Resources include companies across four sectors: 
Industrials, Materials, Energy, and Utilities . We expect the 
segment to generate 10 -15% earnings growth in 2027, 
although Energy is likely to remain the largest drag on overall 
growth due to tougher comparisons and softer energy price 
assumptions for 2027. 
Healthcare & Others  consists primarily of Healthcare and 
Consumer Staples. We expect mid-teen earnings growth for 
the segment in 2027, with a rebound in healthcare earnings 
representing a potential upside surprise. 
Our Overweight sector exposures are drawn from each of the 
four key blocks above. Unlike the last few years , with 
concentrated gains from Tech providing market leadership, 
we see our downgrade in global equities also reflecting a 
broadening in market leadership towards each of the other 
sector blocks apart from Tech.

As structural themes such as AI transition from the rapid 
buildout phase towards monetisation, diversified exposure 
across sectors and regions should become increasingly 
important. 
What are the implications for equity 
opportunistic ideas? 
We have opportunistic ideas on Global High -Dividend 
equities, US healthcare, Euro area banks , MSCI 
Switzerland and Japan banks. The selection represents a 
more defensive stance  currently, with an average beta of 
below 1 relative to global equities (MSCI AC World), but 
attractive drivers nonetheless, for each of the ideas.  
Global High-Dividend offers higher-income equity allocation 
with historically lower volatility than global equities. US 
healthcare is supported by wider adoption of obesity 
treatments, advance s in oncology a nd innovative medical 
devices, that support earnings growth. Euro area banks  
continue to see steady earnings growth adding to their 
excess capital, which is being returned to shareholders via 
attractive dividend and share buyback yields. Japan banks 
continue to enjoy net margin expansion from BoJ policy 
normalisation, as well as corporate governance 
improvements. MSCI Switzerland is a newly initiated idea for 
investors seeking defensive exposure. H ealthcare and 
consumer staples account s for more than half the index 
weight, providing exposure to relatively resilient demand that 
is more insulated from economic cycles and AI trends. It also 
has a sizeable financial sector supported by steady wealth 
management growth and well-capitalised insurers with 
disciplined underwriting and resilient premium income. 
We see the case for more active management  in a mid -
cycle transition, as higher market dispersion should create 
greater opportunities for active sector and stock selection, 
particularly as leadership within equities evolves. 
What are the implications of higher equity 
market volatility?  
A more uncertain political backdrop and p otentially maturing 
growth rates in certain market segments may contribute to 
more frequent bouts of market volatility. Higher volatility 
presents an opportunity to use structured strategies to gain 
exposure to equities. Such strategies may be used to 
generate income or to gain exposure at a discounted entry 
price. Investors can also consider strategies that are 
structured to return principal or partial principal at maturity. 
What alternative strategies are suited to a 
regime of moderating equity market returns?  
Investors can consider alternative strategies such as hedge  
fund strategies or private markets, for those with the capacity

to accept some illiquidity.  
Equity long/short hedge fund  strategies would retain growth 
participation with less market exposure. Fund managers buy 
companies they expect to outperform and short those they 
expect to underperform, seeking to earn the difference 
between the two , and benefit even as total equity market 
returns are moderating. 
Within private markets , private equity provides  a means to 
higher returns with the effects of an illiquidity premium. 
Meanwhile, i nfrastructure offers  differentiated exposure to 
structural growth opportunities, where c ontracted long-term 
revenues reduce dependence on equity-market sentiment. 
What are the implications on fixed income 
and FX markets? 
Bonds: This year’s surge in bond yields means bonds now 
offer a hedge against any equity market weakness. We 
believe the 3 -7-year part of the US bond yield curve offers 
the best hedge against any equity sell -off caused by AI -
related growth worries. This part of the bond curve has priced 
in most Fed rate hike expectations, which could reverse if 
growth concerns return. Meanwhile, we favour US Treasury 
Inflation-Protected Securities (TIPS) as a hedge against any 
fuel price-led inflation scare.  
Credit: For corporate bonds, adopting a defensive stance in 
a volatile environment typically involves: i) increasing credit 
quality, favouring higher-rated issuers; and ii) moving up the 
capital structure, by reducing exposure to subordinated debt 
and favouring secured debt. From a sector perspective, 
large, diversified banks, utilities, and quasi -sovereigns tend 
to be more defensive, particularly relative to cyclical sectors, 
such as metals and mining, exploration, and technology.         
FX: We favour bearish AUD/JPY as a tactical hedge against 
any equity market correction. Review of 21 past S&P500 
correction sessions (2010 -2025) shows AUD weakened 
against USD in 86% of cases, while JPY strengthened 
against USD in 67%, supporting our bearish AUD/JPY 
outlook during any equity market stress.

Document 342: EMEA FX Outlook 2027_ Restrictive policy remains the key FX anchor

Source PDF: all report pdf/EMEA FX Outlook 2027_ Restrictive policy remains the key FX anchor.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 17
Status: ok

### Page 1

EMEA FX should remain supported by restrictive policy and
attractive carry, but performance will increasingly diverge. We
favour HUF, ZAR and TRY, while CZK remains supported and PLN
faces headwinds. Global yields, energy prices, ﬁscal risks and
geopolitics remain key drivers
Chris Turner, Rafal Benecki, Adam Antoniak, Mateusz Sutowicz,
Peter Virovacz, David Havrlant, Dmitry Dolgin, Valentin Tataru,
Stefan Posea, Muhammet Mercan and Frantisek Taborsky

Executive summary
Central banks across EMEA remain focused on currency stability as
inﬂation, ﬁscal risks and geopolitics keep the external environment
challenging. Restrictive monetary policy and attractive carry
support several currencies, particularly the forint, rand and lira,
while solid external balances and EU inﬂows provide additional
buﬀers. At the same time, high global yields, energy price risks,
ﬁscal concerns and geopolitical uncertainty should drive greater

diﬀerentiation across the region.
The CEE3 region, comprising the Czech Republic, Hungary and
Poland, should remain relatively well supported by restrictive
monetary policy, although the outlook is becoming more

diﬀerentiated. The Hungarian forint remains our preferred Central
European currency, with the National Bank of Hungary expected to
keep rates at 5.50% for the next 9-12 months, while ﬁscal
consolidation, EU-fund inﬂows and an improving external balance
should keep the forint biased towards the stronger end of its 350-
370 EUR/HUF range. The Czech koruna should also beneﬁt from
positive nominal and real rate diﬀerentials after the Czech National
Bank raised rates to 4%, although weaker growth should prevent a

renewed hiking cycle. By contrast, we have turned mildly negative
on the Polish zloty. Narrower rate diﬀerentials, ﬁscal concerns and
rising political and rating risks should limit PLN performance,
despite strong economic growth and sizeable EU inﬂows. Overall,
global yields, energy prices and geopolitics are likely to remain
important drivers across the region.

In the managed currency segment in EMEA, the Romanian leu

should see greater stability after the volatility of the past two
years. Fiscal consolidation, diminishing rating risks and lower
government borrowing needs have improved Romania’s
fundamental story, while weak growth and falling inﬂation should
allow the National Bank of Romania to start cutting rates next year.
However, the central bank appears more willing to tolerate FX
volatility than in the past, suggesting that stability around current
EUR/RON levels should not be mistaken for a return to the

### Page 3

exceptionally tight management seen historically. Meanwhile, the
Central Bank of Turkey should continue its gradual easing cycle
while maintaining suﬃciently attractive real rates to contain
dollarisation and support reserves. Although disinﬂation has been
delayed and inﬂation risks remain tilted to the upside, renewed
reserve accumulation and contained FX demand suggest the
current exchange-rate regime remains ﬁrmly in place. We therefore
expect the lira to continue oﬀering attractive carry despite ongoing

nominal depreciation.
On the periphery of the EMEA region, South Africa’s rand should
remain well supported by a hawkish central bank and attractive
carry. With inﬂation pressures elevated, the South African Reserve
Bank is keeping policy ﬁrmly restrictive, while relatively low
volatility has reinforced the rand’s appeal. Progress on energy and
freight reforms could provide an additional tailwind in 2027,
particularly if the external environment improves, leaving the rand

among the stronger performers in our EMEA forecast. Israel’s shekel
faces a more balanced outlook. The Bank of Israel has responded to
low inﬂation and excessive currency strength with rate cuts and FX
intervention, signalling limited appetite for further appreciation.
Softer US tech performance could also weigh on investment and
hedging ﬂows. However, Israel’s persistent current account surplus,
strong reserve position and solid credit fundamentals should limit

depreciation, while any improvement in geopolitics would provide
renewed support.

EUR/PLN: Turning negative on the zloty
Spot Year ahead bias 4Q26 1Q27 2Q27 3Q27
EUR/PLN 4.40 Mildly Bearish 4.39 4.36 4.34 4.33
We are shifting our view on the zloty from neutral to mildly
negative: The latest weakening of the zloty was driven mainly by global
factors: a hawkish Fed, high US Treasury yields, a stronger dollar and
concerns over French public ﬁnances. Rate hikes by both the Fed and

the ECB have also narrowed Poland's interest rate advantage, eroding
Your privacy is important
At ING we respect your right to choose how your data is used.
You can review and change your preferences at any time in
our Cookie policy.
Essential
Performance Cookies
Targeting Cookies

### Page 4

PLN’s carry appeal. Solid fundamentals should nonetheless prevent a
disorderly depreciation: Poland remains one of the fastest-growing EU
economies, its external position is sound, signiﬁcant inﬂows from the
Recovery and Resilience Facility (RRF) and the Security Action for
Europe (SAFE) defence programme are still ahead, and foreign
investors' exposure to Polish bonds is light. Moreover, the National Bank
of Poland (NBP) may tighten monetary policy in early 2027. However,

many of these factors are already priced in.
Risks to the zloty are primarily domestic and geopolitical: Several
downside risks for PLN have intensiﬁed. Public ﬁnances remain the key
vulnerability: the ﬁscal deﬁcit is the largest in the EU, public debt is
rising and approaching statutory thresholds, and no meaningful
consolidation is planned before the 2027 parliamentary elections.
Tense cohabitation between the government and the President limits

the scope for ﬁscal repair, while the escalating dispute over the
Constitutional Tribunal adds to the risk premium. Rating pressure is
mounting, with one rating downgrade already delivered this year and
the second outlook revised to negative. Geopolitically, high energy
prices linked to the Middle East conﬂict, escalation of the war in Ukraine
and hybrid attacks on Europe remain the main threats.
We expect EUR/PLN to remain in a 4.32-4.45 range over the coming

months: In the near term, risks are skewed towards a weaker zloty, as a
negative rating action or an escalation of domestic political tensions
could push the pair towards the upper bound. A gradual recovery of the
zloty in 2027 would require sustainably lower energy prices, lower
global yields, a ﬁrmer stance from the NBP, and continued EU-fund
inﬂows. Even then, loose ﬁscal policy ahead of the elections makes a
return to the stronger levels seen in the ﬁrst half of this year unlikely.

EUR/HUF: Rangebound, with a stronger-forint
bias
Spot Year ahead bias 4Q26 1Q27 2Q27 3
EUR/HUF 364.00 Neutral 360.00 365.00 365.00 36
The forint remains a prisoner of global forces: With the ﬁscal
consolidation plan presented and preparations for the EU’s Exchange
Rate Mechanism (ERM) II talks underway, there is no longer a single
domestic event that can be circled as the next major market trigger.
Instead, energy prices, geopolitics and global risk appetite are likely to

dominate. The forint also remains an important part of the National
Your privacy is important

### Page 5

Bank of Hungary’s (NBH) inﬂation strategy, especially given the high
degree of exchange rate pass-through. In this volatile environment, we
expect rates to remain on hold for the next 9–12 months at 5.50%.
Inﬂation may provide room for easing, but that room remains behind a
closed door. Only a sustained and structural improvement in the risk
environment is likely to provide the key.
EUR/HUF should remain contained within a 350-370 range: At the
stronger end, 350 represents a natural line in the sand. A signiﬁcant

decline in Hungary’s risk premium could take the pair towards this level,
but the accompanying dovish repricing would make a sustained break
lower diﬃcult. Markets are likely to bring forward expectations of rate
cuts, reﬂecting the Monetary Council’s underlying dovish bias, a view we
share. At the weaker end, the central bank’s readiness to use the
exchange rate channel should limit any disorderly sell-oﬀ. A non-linear
move towards or beyond 370 could trigger verbal intervention and, if

necessary, direct market action. We see an actual policy rate hike as a
last resort, with the central bank likely to reach for other interest rate
tools, including the FX swap rate, before touching the base rate.
Credible intervention does not always require the central bank to pull
the trigger; if markets believe the threat, they will do much of the
heavy lifting themselves.
Domestic factors should keep the forint on the stronger side: Without
a singular deﬁning event, markets will closely monitor monthly budget

execution, inﬂation developments, and progress in ﬁscal consolidation.
Actual EU-fund inﬂows, an expected return of the current account to
surplus and the possibility of positive sovereign rating actions should
keep the forint closer to the stronger end of its range. A gradual grind
lower in EUR/HUF could even bring 350 into view at moments of peak
optimism. However, such strength would probably be followed by
monetary easing, preventing a lasting appreciation beyond that level.

We therefore see the forint primarily as a tactical market within a
longer-term constructive story, supported by Hungary’s potential path
towards ERM II entry at the start of 2029.
EUR/CZK: Koruna holds up well in tough times
Spot Year ahead bias 4Q26 1Q27 2Q27 3Q27
EUR/CZK 24.38 Neutral 24.30 24.26 24.22 24.19
This is not a hiking cycle: The Czech National Bank (CNB) increased the
policy rate to 4% in two steps yet stressed repeatedly that this doesn’t

### Page 6

represent the onset of another hiking cycle. Moreover, the tone of the
latest press conferences was relatively dovish, while the meeting with
analysts revealed that the country’s dwindling economic performance
is a growing concern for some board members. We saw few pushbacks
against the idea that the equilibrium rate is likely higher than generally
assumed. In turn, it seems we’re in the process of searching for the right
spot in a world of growing uncertainty. A weaker koruna contributing to

rising import prices will be one of the key factors that the CNB will
watch when it comes to maintaining price stability over the next year,
but interest rates should remain the policy tool of choice. Stable rates
are the most likely outcome if our forecast for next year’s average
inﬂation of 3.1% is right.
Constrained consumers: The second-round eﬀects linked to the
protracted Hormuz shock have started to materialise and will be felt in
renewed annual increases in regulated prices and food prices. Large

distributors are beginning to announce increases in energy and heating
prices, while higher water charges will also play their part. With food
prices also set to rise soon, there is little chance for any relaxation of
the monetary policy stance, unless household consumption and
economic performance prove weaker than expected. Indeed,
disappointing real GDP ﬁgures could put a lid on a potential hiking spree.
With real wage growth softening materially over the next year and

borrowing costs rising, many households would be forced to reconsider
their spending habits, especially when it comes to discretionary
spending.
Koruna enjoys rate support: Still, we will continue to see a positive
interest rate diﬀerential with the ECB in both nominal and real terms,
which makes the koruna attractive. The domestic currency is set to
remain stable in times of elevated uncertainty, while a gradual
strengthening trajectory is likely once the global turmoil shows signs of

settling down. Should oil prices recede further and remain lower for
longer, we would see fresh wind in koruna’s sails, as this would prepare
the ground for the Czech economic expansion to accelerate. Real
convergence would get back on track, and the more pronounced
outperformance relative to the eurozone’s real growth would once
again work in koruna’s favour. Moreover, the CNB is free to raise rates as
it pleases should domestic or imported inﬂation get out of hand; we

have some doubts about whether the ECB has the same room for
manoeuvre, as it must, like it or not, keep one eye on French bond
yields.

activity and improving headline inﬂation would justify an early start,
but sticky price pressures on the services front, potential episodes of

political uncertainty and risk-oﬀ global events could delay the easing
cycle. In this context, we think that liquidity management will remain
an important part of NBR’s balancing act. The liquidity surplus fell
sharply through October, from around RON20.8bn to RON4.9bn,
supporting tighter money market conditions during the recent period of
intense FX pressures. However, FX-implied yields have since retreated
from an early-October peak to more manageable levels, broadly in line

with their one-year average. Acute pressure on the currency seems to
have eased since mid-October, although it’s not excluded that a smaller
liquidity surplus going forward will continue to support monetary
transmission and discourage renewed short positioning.
The domestic slowdown has yet to translate into a convincing
correction of Romania’s external imbalance: Retail sales have been
contracting for more than a year, reﬂecting weaker household demand,

ﬁscal consolidation and elevated inﬂation. Nevertheless, the January-
September trade deﬁcit remained visibly elevated at 6.3% of GDP in
2026, only marginally narrower than 6.5% in 2025 and 6.7% in 2024 for
the same period. The investment cycle has also sustained imports
despite weaker consumption. In particular, the ﬁnal push to complete
RRF-funded infrastructure, transport, energy and healthcare projects
has generated strong demand for imported machinery, equipment and

intermediate inputs. We think that Romania’s potential growth
currently sits at around 2.0%, but productive investment could lift it
towards 2.4-2.7% in 2027-2029 through better infrastructure,
connectivity and energy availability, which should favour the medium
and long-term fundamentals for RON.
Our baseline is for further controlled depreciation in 2027, with ﬁscal
credibility, political stability and capital ﬂows as key factors to
watch: Romania’s investment-grade rating preservation has provided

some relief, but the cautious approach of markets with regards to ﬁscal
implementation and the delivery of a credible medium-term
consolidation plan remains in place. In this context, uncertainties over

Romania’s capacity to absorb the remaining €6.4bn of RRF funds

expect EUR/RON to remain around 5.35 by end-2026 and move
gradually towards 5.45 by end-2027. Renewed episodes of political

faster depreciation.
EUR/RSD: Stability is likely to prevail
Spot Year ahead bias 4Q26 1Q27 2Q27 3
EUR/RSD 118.00 Neutral 117.50 117.40 117.30 11
Active FX management should remain the main anchor for the
dinar: The National Bank of Serbia (NBS) surprised with a 25bp hike to
6.0% at the November meeting, seeking to contain second-round
inﬂation eﬀects from persistently high energy prices, while also leaning

against the recent depreciation pressure on the dinar. Our baseline
outlook forecasts no further hikes at this stage, and we expect 50bp of
rate cuts in 2027 (likely through the second half of the year), taking the
policy rate to 5.50% by end-2027. Nevertheless, we think that the
balance tilts more towards upside risks for rates, rather than to the
downside. Currency stability has been a key policy feature this year and
is likely set to remain an integral part of the policy framework ahead;

the NBS sold €475m between January and September, including
substantial intervention in September, when it sold €380m as energy-
related FX demand increased. In any case, reserves remain
comfortable, giving the central bank ample capacity to contain
volatility moving forwards.
A stronger domestic economy should support the dinar, although
ﬁscal stimulus and investment-related imports create external and
inﬂationary trade-oﬀs: Serbia’s GDP expanded by 4.0% year-on-year in

3Q26, accelerating from 3.8% in the second quarter and 3.2% in the
ﬁrst. Private consumption, a resilient labour market and the investment
programme ahead of EXPO 2027 should continue to support activity
ahead. However, the more expansionary ﬁscal stance, infrastructure
spending and the import-intensive nature of the investment cycle could
also act as catalysts for slightly stronger price pressures and trade
deﬁcits in the near term. That said, at around 43% of GDP, public debt

We expect EUR/RSD to remain broadly stable around 117.4-117.5

With substantial reserves and a demonstrated commitment to FX

stability, the NBS should be able to absorb further temporary pressures
and keep the currency close to its desired range.
USD/UAH: Orderly hryvnia depreciation
Spot Year ahead bias 4Q26 1Q27 2Q27 3Q27
USD/UAH 45.44 Neutral 45.20 45.30 45.40 45.50
Gradual UAH depreciation ahead: The hryvnia has weakened to record
lows against the US dollar. Even so, we expect further gradual
depreciation. Pressure stems from a ﬁrmer dollar on the back of a
hawkish Fed and renewed tension in the Middle East as well as

intensiﬁed Russian attacks on Ukraine's economy. The National Bank of
Ukraine (NBU) continues its strategy of "managed ﬂexibility": it
tolerates an orderly slide but intervenes heavily in the FX market to
prevent disorderly moves. Monetary policy has turned tighter, with a
series of rate hikes since the summer reaching 150bp in total
accompanied by guidance that rates will stay high well into 2027, as
inﬂation has turned into double-digit territory recently.

FX reserves have declined: External ﬁnancing remains the key anchor
for the hryvnia. However, international reserves have fallen markedly
from their record highs earlier in the year, as FX interventions and debt
service outpaced inﬂows. On one hand, disbursements under the EU's
Ukraine Support Loan have lagged behind plans because of delays in
agreed reforms, and Brussels has made further funding conditional on

their implementation. On the other hand, according to NBU data, the FX

Focus on peace negotiations: The macroeconomic outlook has
deteriorated signiﬁcantly because of the Russian war, but direct
technical peace talks between Ukraine and Russia with US participation
oﬀer some hope of an end to the war. Intensiﬁed attacks on industry,

pushed the economy back into contraction this year, while renewed
inﬂationary pressure is eroding real incomes. Labour shortages linked to
mobilisation and rising production costs continue to weigh on
competitiveness, and any recovery in 2027 is likely to be slow. Against
this backdrop, we expect USD/UAH to drift gradually higher, towards the

accept as long as it remains orderly. Timely EU and IMF disbursements

are the main upside factor, while a ceaseﬁre with Russia, which would
change the picture materially, does not look imminent despite
intensiﬁed US diplomatic eﬀorts.
USD/KZT: Carry can cushion external risks
Spot Year ahead bias 4Q26 1Q27 2Q27 3
USD/KZT 443.00 Bullish 450.00 440.00 450.00 46
High real rates should survive NBK easing cycle: The tenge enters 2027
with monetary policy still favouring portfolio inﬂows. The National Bank
of the Republic of Kazakhstan (NBK) started cutting rates in 2026 to

15.75% currently, as inﬂation moderated to 9.2% YoY and the currency
strengthened, but the policy stance remained cautious, with cuts
lagging the actual CPI slowdown. We expect the easing cycle to remain
gradual, leaving real rates high by regional and EM standards. This
matters increasingly for KZT as foreign participation in Kazakhstan's
local debt has been a key driver of the tenge's strength this year. The
main risk to our constructive view would be a faster easing cycle than

we expect, particularly if inﬂation proves stickier and reduces the real-
rate cushion. Conversely, sustained disinﬂation accompanied by
cautious rate cuts should preserve attractive carry and keep monetary
policy an important source of support for KZT.
Capital ﬂows are a new strength, but also a new sensitivity: One of
the biggest changes in the KZT story over the last 12 months has been
the growing role of foreign portfolio ﬂows. Progress towards Euroclear

connectivity, positive sovereign rating momentum, increased domestic

### Page 11

debt issuance and high real yields have helped attract foreign investors
into KZT government bonds. At the same time, regular state-related FX
sales have fallen sharply. This makes the tenge more market-driven,
but also more exposed to changes in global rates and risk appetite.
After its strong rally, we see limited room for another large
appreciation. However, downside risks are also cushioned: a $2.2bn
backlog of National Fund-related FX sales accumulated between June

and October gives the authorities a potentially signiﬁcant supply buﬀer
should private inﬂows reverse. We therefore expect KZT to remain
resilient in 2027 even if global portfolio ﬂows become less supportive.
Resilience does not equal structural appreciation: Higher core yields or
a broader global risk-oﬀ episode, AI-led or otherwise, could weaken
foreign demand for KZT assets and put pressure on the currency. This is
increasingly relevant as carry-driven ﬂows become a bigger part of the

KZT story. But we would not expect an indiscriminate sell-oﬀ; still-high
real rates and the accumulated backlog of state FX sales provide
meaningful buﬀers against a reversal in private inﬂows, while the role of
oil should not be overestimated as export constraints, growing imports
and large dividend outﬂows prevented the tenge from beneﬁtting
materially from the recent oil price rally. Our constructive 2027 view
should therefore not be confused with a structurally bullish view on KZT.

Kazakhstan's persistent current account deﬁcit remains consistent with
gradual depreciation over the longer term. For 2027, however, we
expect domestic buﬀers to keep any depreciation contained even
under a less supportive global backdrop.
USD/UZS: Domestic buffers should keep the
soum resilient
Spot Year ahead bias 4Q26 1Q27 2Q
USD/UZS 11974.00 Neutral 11770.00 11828.00 1188
High real rates are here to stay: Uzbekistan enters 2027 with
monetary policy providing a substantial buﬀer against global volatility.

The Central Bank of Uzbekistan (CBU) kept its policy rate at 14.00% in
November and turned more cautious on inﬂation, raising its year-end
forecast to around 7% amid strong domestic demand, utility tariﬀ
increases and external food and energy risks. This makes near-term
easing unlikely, but we still see scope for cautious cuts in 2027 if ﬁscal
restraint continues and inﬂation pressures subside. Crucially for the
soum, easing should not require sacriﬁcing the sizeable real-rate

cushion. This should help retain carry support even if global rates

remain elevated. The main risk would be a combination of sticky
inﬂation and weaker FX, which would leave the central bank with less
room to cut and potentially require real rates to remain higher for
longer.
Gold export backlog provides an additional buﬀer if capital ﬂows
weaken: The soum's resilience this year is particularly notable because
it has received relatively little help from Uzbekistan's traditional source
of FX support. Only 18 tonnes of gold were exported in January-

September 2026, compared with 85 tonnes during the same period of
2025, despite much stronger gold prices. Instead, the soum's strength
appears to have been driven largely by capital inﬂows. That creates
both a vulnerability and a potential buﬀer for 2027. Higher global yields
or a broader risk-oﬀ episode could weaken carry-driven ﬂows, but a
recovery in physical gold shipments towards Uzbekistan's historical 80-
120 tonne annual range would bring additional FX supply into the

market. Combined with still-high real rates and continued reform-
related capital inﬂows, this should limit the soum's vulnerability to a
deterioration in global sentiment.
It is too early to consider the old depreciation trend defeated: We
therefore remain constructive on UZS for 2027, but like KZT, would
distinguish resilience from a permanently stronger currency. Continued
ﬁscal consolidation is helpful, with the deﬁcit having fallen from 4.7% of

GDP in 2023 to below 2% currently, but Uzbekistan continues to run
twin deﬁcits and somewhat elevated inﬂation risks. Higher core yields
or a global risk-oﬀ episode could still weaken carry support and put
depreciation pressure on UZS, although high real rates and catch-up
gold exports provide meaningful buﬀers against a large adjustment.
Over the longer term, we would therefore remain cautious about
extrapolating recent soum strength. For 2027, however, the

combination of restrictive real rates, ﬁscal restraint and potentially
stronger gold-related FX inﬂows should keep depreciation contained
even under a less supportive global backdrop.

Inﬂation path delayed in 2027: October inﬂation in Turkey came in
slightly above expectations, interrupting the recent downtrend. Even

so, we see annual inﬂation ﬁnishing the year below 30%. In the near

Prolonged US-Iran tensions could quickly intensify price pressures,
particularly as the sliding scale mechanism continues to unwind. The
latest Medium-Term Programme raised the 2027 forecast to 21%, up

remains optimistic compared with the 23.3% expectation reﬂected in

the Market Participants Survey. The upward adjustment mainly reﬂects
both the carry-over eﬀect of stronger-than-anticipated inﬂation this
year on next year’s starting point and the inﬂuence of global economic
conditions. Overall, the new forecast suggests that the disinﬂation
process under the stabilisation programme launched in mid-2023 has
been pushed further out. We forecast 26% next year, with risks to the
upside.
CBT signals gradual easing: In its latest MPC statement, the Central

Bank of Turkey (CBT) adjusted its language, shifting attention from
“monetary policy decisions” to the scale and timing of future policy
actions. This change suggests that, unless the factors behind the
current easing cycle change materially, the Bank is likely to continue
lowering rates in December. From this perspective, October inﬂation is
unlikely to signiﬁcantly alter the policy outlook. The main risks for
policy remain developments in the Middle East and any deterioration in

the disinﬂation trend. At the same time, the Bank is expected to keep a
close watch on domestic demand, international reserve dynamics, and
depositor behaviour when assessing future policy moves. Maintaining
suﬃciently attractive real interest rates is likely to remain important in
limiting pressure on foreign exchange reserves. Against this backdrop,
we continue to expect the easing cycle to move forward gradually, with
the policy rate reaching 30% by end-2027.

Reserve accumulation resumes in early November: Investment fund
volatility has had only a limited eﬀect on dollarisation across deposits
and mutual funds, although lira-denominated funds experienced
outﬂows as some investors appeared to reduce money market fund
positions. At the same time, growth in TRY deposits and repo accounts
indicates that dollarization pressures have remained contained. After
accumulating more than $30bn through net foreign exchange

purchases between June and August, the CBT saw both its gross
reserves and net FX position excluding swaps decline in September and
October, largely reﬂecting turbulence in investment funds. However, the
Bank’s net FX position rose sharply in early November, pointing to a
return of non-resident inﬂows alongside subdued foreign currency
demand from residents. Additionally, although the CBT increased the
pace of FX depreciation lately, this appears to be a technical

USD/ZAR: Hawkish SARB and key reforms in

Spot Year ahead bias 4Q26 1Q27 2Q27 3Q
USD/ZAR 16.66 Mildly Bearish 16.50 16.50 16.25 16
South African Reserve Bank stays hawkish: Having adopted a 3%
inﬂation target in 2025, the SARB has already hiked twice this year. With
fuel prices looking like they will stay high, the SARB is expecting

headline inﬂation to stay above 5% and is also concerned about
inﬂation expectations becoming unanchored. The risk of higher food
prices from El Niño drought conditions later this year is not helping
either. But even with growth contracting in the second quarter, the SARB
is clearly taking no chances with inﬂation. The market now expects
another 75bp of tightening (to 8.00%) over the next 12 months and
policy to remain ﬁrmly in restrictive territory. High implied yields and

relatively (by the rand’s standards) low volatility have made the rand
one of the better EMFX performers this year.
2027 will be a big year for reforms: Increasingly, we are hearing about
the need for legislated reforms to be implemented. These largely focus
on the energy and freight sectors. On the former, Eskom’s ability to
avoid further load-shedding, increase generation, improve its ﬁnances
and integrate the private sector will be key. On the latter, whether 11

private train companies can start using Transnet infrastructure to
dramatically increase freight volumes will be a key test. The good news
is that the Government for National Unity (GNU) seems to be ﬁnding a
way through thorny issues – e.g., ﬁnally passing the 2025 budget – and
the next national elections are not due until 2029. Consensus currently
expects South African growth in the 1.5-2.0% area.
Local assets have withstood external pressure well: Local asset

markets seem to have withstood the strong dollar and the US Treasury
sell-oﬀ relatively well. One-month USD/ZAR traded option prices have
been happy to trade at low, sub-10% levels since the summer. And
South Africa’s credit default swap remains relatively calm near +130bp,
perhaps because of the country’s primary ﬁscal surplus and a more
manageable current account deﬁcit of less than 2% of GDP.
Additionally, despite the ﬂows into South Africa last year after the SARB

adopted the 3% inﬂation target, foreigners only hold 25% of available

USD/ILS: Bank of Israel gets what it wants
Spot Year ahead bias 4Q26 1Q27 2Q27 3Q27 4
USD/ILS 3.18 Mildly Bearish 3.15 3.15 3.05 3.05 3
Bank of Israel ﬁghts shekel strength: Having traded as low as 2.80 in

late May, USD/ILS looks to be heading into year-end well above 3.00.
Yes, the strong dollar has played a role here, but so has the Bank of
Israel (BoI). This year, it has cut rates by 100bp to 3.25% and also
intervened to buy USD/ILS back in May. The BoI’s dovish position has
been justiﬁed by CPI continuing to surprise on the downside. CPI has
been below the middle of the BoI’s target range (1-3%) all year. Yet we
suspect the BoI is more worried about the strong shekel from a growth

perspective. Here, the real, trade-weighted shekel remains 30% above
its 20-year average and will prove to be a headwind to Israeli exporters.
While Washington may want trading partners to step back from FX
intervention against local currency strength, it may see Israel as a
special case.
Shekel joined at the hip with tech: Part of the recent shekel weakness
may reﬂect weakness in the US tech sector. Strength there normally
helps the shekel via two channels: investment ﬂows and hedging. On

the former, a tougher IPO environment may well be weighing on ﬂows
into Israel. During good times, these ﬂows can be worth as much as
$5bn per quarter through the Israeli current account. On the latter, a big
fall in tech can see Israeli investors reduce their hedges on falling
overseas portfolios. For example, in the second quarter, Israeli pension
funds and insurers sold $18bn of FX against the shekel as they increased
FX hedges on booming tech portfolios. The reverse may be happening

now and leaves the shekel a little vulnerable if the US tech story has
further to correct.
Good-quality credit and current account surplus help: While the BoI
may not want USD/ILS to go too much below 3.00, that does not mean
USD/ILS needs to rally a lot either. Israel is seen as a solid A- sovereign
credit and has very strong FX reserve coverage of short-term debt and
imports. Its budget deﬁcit of 4-5% of GDP is seen as manageable, and

FINANCIAL CONDITIONS IN THE EUROZONE: A TIGHTENING TEMPORARILY TEMPERED BY CREDIT VOLUMES
Thomas Humblot
*LATEST OBSERVATION: 5 OCTOBER 2026  **ESTIMATE
Since June 2026, the ECB has been publishing 1 a new indicator that helps to assess the transmission of its monetary policy to the economy:
the Broad Intermediation Gauge 2 (ECB-BIG). This new tool has revealed that, thus far, strong credit growth has limited the restrictive impact

of tightening financial conditions caused by rising interest rates in the Eurozone. However, this offsetting effect is beginning to fade.
The ECB-BIG: a new indicator for a broader view of financial conditions
The ECB-BIG complements the range of indicators used by the European Central Bank (ECB) to assess the effective transmission of its monetary
policy. This new tool provides a comprehensive view of how restrictive (or expansive) financial conditions are in the Eurozone. It stands out

for its approach that focuses more specifically on intermediation conditions. Therefore, the ECB-BIG provides a more comprehensive view than
most other financial condition indicators of financial conditions. In fact, it covers variables relating to the volume of bank lending to households
and businesses (the ‘volume’ component). Traditionally, this type of indicator tends to focus more on market variables (primarily interest and
exchange rates), share prices and corporate spreads. In addition, the ‘price’ component of the ECB-BIG is extended to include the cost of bank

loans, which is another new feature.
The rise in interest rates was temporarily offset by increased lending
By aggregating the variables relating to banking and non-banking intermediation conditions, the ECB-BIG shows that rising interest rates
(negative price effect) were offset in particular by lending volumes, which have continued to rise in 2026 (positive volume effect). A more
‘traditional’ indicator of financial conditions, the Macro-Finance FCI (MF-FCI) — which is, incidentally, included in the ECB-BIG — reveals a

much more pronounced tightening over the same period (see the orange curve in the chart). However, this offsetting of the price effect by the
volume effect is now beginning to wane, a sign that monetary policy is being effectively transmitted to the economy. Financial intermediaries
are gradually passing on the rise in rates to their customers (see the dotted green lines, which represent the composite indicators of the cost
of new loans to households and businesses, combining interest rates for different maturities), while lending volumes are falling. As a result,

between July and August 2026, seasonally adjusted flows of loans to households and businesses almost halved.
1 P . Lane, 2026, The Outlook for the Euro Area Economy, Background slides for Reuters NEXT Europe, London, 16 June 2026
2 A. Allayioti et al., 2026, The ECB-BIG index: tracking credit conditions in the euro area, ECB Economic Bulletin, Issue 5/2026
SOURCE: ALLAYIOTI AND AL. (2026), ECB, BLETZINGER ET AL. (2026), BNP PARIBAS ESTIMATES
Credit volumes have temporarily cushioned the rise in interest rates

In the longer term, financial conditions are expected to tighten, including as measured by the ECB-BIG
Despite the very sharp widening of sovereign spreads between 28 September and 2 October, financial conditions as measured by the
MF-FCI did not tighten, with sovereign yields actually falling over this period (with the exception of the OAT and the BTP), for example.

Nevertheless, the high volatility of the bond market, particularly in the sovereign segment, is likely to continue to affect financial
conditions. Looking slightly further ahead, the ECB’s key interest rate rises — the one that occurred on 16 September and another that we
anticipate for December 2026 (both +25 bp) — are likely to result in a more pronounced tightening. With a time lag of a number of weeks,
financial conditions, as defined by the ECB-BIG, are also expected to tighten. The price effect is likely to lead to a negative volume effect

on this occasion.
thomas.humblot@bnpparibas.com

Document 353: FX Daily_ EUR_USD gears up for a 1.10 test

Source PDF: all report pdf/FX Daily_ EUR_USD gears up for a 1.10 test.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

The dollar’s strong rally this week is ﬁrmly rooted in a shift in
short-term rate dynamics, driven by hawkish Fed rhetoric and
hotter inﬂation. That makes it harder to argue for a material
rebound in EUR/USD, which may instead test 1.10 should
upcoming data releases oﬀer the dollar extra support. For now,
we don’t see the conditions for a break below 1.10

Frantisek Taborsky, Francesco Pesole and Chris Turner
EUR/USD could test 1.10 if upcoming data provides further support for the
dollar
USD: Hawkish skew ahead
The combination of hawkish Fedspeak and a US CPI beat earlier this
week continues to pay dividends for the dollar. DXY is now back at
Liberation Day (April 2025) levels, with real front-end rate
diﬀerentials continuing to fuel strong dollar momentum.
Importantly, this remains a rates-driven move, with contained
excessive USD strength relative to short-term fair value currently
built in.

### Page 2

In our estimates, stretched undervaluation vs USD is only visible
against the euro, consistent with the French-related premium, and
SEK, which tends to underperform fundamentals during hawkish
repricing as both an activity currency and a low yielder.
This environment is also preventing high-beta currencies from
drawing any beneﬁts from the extension of the equity rebound. The
resilience to both Nvidia’s idiosyncratic losses and the repricing
higher in interest rates is sending a rather strong signal of solidity in

the AI trade at the moment.
We continue to see upside risks for the dollar in the coming days.
The Fed's Chris Waller has put a 50bp December move on the radar,
and that should keep markets biased towards hawkish
interpretations of incoming data. December pricing remains at
28bp, leaving scope for further adjustment. Today’s home sales
data are unlikely to matter, but tomorrow’s PPI will. That may be
the catalyst for another USD leg higher.
Francesco Pesole

EUR: ECB members unlikely to match
Fedspeak
If, on the other side of the Atlantic, Chris Waller – previously
considered dovish-leaning – opened the door to larger hikes, in the
eurozone a hawkish-leaning voice, Gabriel Makhlouf, called for
greater caution on tightening yesterday, stressing price pressures
remain contained to energy for now.
That contrast neatly captures the current EUR/USD story. The pair's
two-year swap rate diﬀerential has widened by around 15bp over

the past ﬁve sessions, and we think the scope for further repricing is
greater in front-end USD rates than EUR rates in the near term.
That leaves EUR/USD at material risk of testing 1.100 in the coming
days. We expect that level to oﬀer relatively solid support, not least
because the pair is already trading at a nearly 2% discount to our
short-term fair value. A decisive move below 1.100 would probably
require markets to seriously entertain a 50bp December hike. That

looks like a stretch for now.

### Page 3

Francesco Pesole
RON: Elevated inﬂation will keep the
NBR unchanged for a longer
October inﬂation data for Romania was released this morning – the
ﬁnal set of ﬁgures from the CEE region to be published in recent
days. Headline inﬂation rose from 6.6% to 6.9%, in line with market
expectations. Inﬂation is expected to remain at these levels until the
end of the year, only beginning to decline next year. NBR is set to
make a decision on interest rates today. However, given the current

inﬂation proﬁle, no changes are anticipated. Rates are expected to
remain unchanged at 6.50%, and we don’t expect change until
2Q27. In recent months, the NBR has provided very little forward
guidance in its statements, so it would be a surprise to see
anything new from the central bank.
The EUR/RON exchange rate has stabilised slightly above 5.340,
following some volatility over the previous two months. Data for
October indicates that excess liquidity in the banking sector fell

from RON30bn to zero. Nevertheless, we expect a recovery in
liquidity for the remainder of the year, driven by anticipated inﬂows
of EU funds and the government’s recent FX issuance. The overall
balance should return to surplus. FX implied yields have normalised
signiﬁcantly following the spike in October, though they remain
slightly elevated. In our view, the return of liquidity to the system
should push implied rates toward the lower bound of the NBR’s

interest rate corridor, closer to 5.50%.
Frantisek Taborsky
CEE: Energy rebound and stronger dollar
keep FX under pressure
Poland returns to the market today following yesterday’s holiday-
related closure, and we should see a signiﬁcant catch-up in the
rates market. Following a rebound in global energy prices, we
observed a rise in rates across the CZK and HUF markets, roughly 5-
8bp along the entire curve. At the same time, CEE FX has come

under renewed pressure due to energy prices and the US dollar

This aligns with our bearish view on CEE FX, and we maintain this
bias. Aside from data releases and a central bank meeting in
Romania, the regional calendar is empty today, leaving the market
driven primarily by the broader global narrative. On Friday, we will
see regional GDP data and the minutes from the CNB meeting,
which could provide fresh impetus to the local story. Until then,

however, the direction remains unchanged, and we may see further
downside for CEE currencies.
Frantisek Taborsky

Document 389: NZ labour market review chartpack, Q3 2026 (2026-11-12)

Source PDF: all report pdf/NZ labour market review chartpack, Q3 2026 (2026-11-12).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 14
Status: ok

### Page 1

LABOUR MARKET CHARTPACK
4 Nov 2026

### Page 2

Westpac New Zealand Economics
2
Michael Gordon, Senior Economist | +64 9 336 5670 | michael.gordon@westpac.co.nz      Connect with us
Key points – September quarter 2026
• The September quarter surveys pointed to ongoing softness in labour market conditions. The unemployment rate rose from 5.6% to 5.7%, continuing an
almost five-year run of increases. The unemployment rate is now at its highest level since September 2015.
• There were mixed messages in terms of jobs growth: the Household Labour Force Survey reported a 0.1% fall in employment, while the employer-

focused Quarterly Employment Survey suggested a modest rise. The Monthly Employment Indicator had also pointed to a small rise in jobs.
• The fall in HLFS employment was accompanied by a fall in the participation rate. These co-movements essentially reversed the surprisingly large
increases that we saw in the June quarter, which we had suggested at the time were more likely due to sampling error in the survey rather than a
genuine surge.
• We generally recommend focusing on the unemployment rate, which is less vulnerable to sampling errors – and the message here is one of softness. Our

best interpretation is that, to the extent that jobs are growing, it’s not fast enough to absorb the growth in the population.
• While the economy has returned to growth over the last year or so, hiring activity remains weak. This may be an ongoing legacy of employment trends in
previous years, when firms were relatively reluctant to lay people off during the slowdown, which then potentially left them overstaffed even as activity
started to pick up again. It’s not clear how long this effect can persist, though.

• Wage growth remains muted. Although cost-of-living pressures have increased on workers, the existing degree of spare capacity in the labour market
doesn’t give them much leverage to negotiate larger pay increases.
• Overall, we judge these results to be weaker than the RBNZ expected, in particular the rise in the unemployment rate. This data supports the views of
those on the policy committee who were less convinced in October about the potential for second-round inflation pressures in the economy.

### Page 3

Westpac New Zealand Economics
3
Employment
• The number of people employed fell by 0.1% in the September quarter, according to the
Household Labour Force Survey (HLFS). Previous quarters were revised down a little,
reflecting downward revisions to the working-age population estimates. Annual employment
growth slowed from 0.9% to 0.7%.
• This was substantially softer than our forecast of a 0.5% rise for the quarter. Our forecast
was based on the Monthly Employment Indicator (MEI), a comprehensive measure that is

drawn from income tax data. While there are some differences in coverage for these two
measures, any significant divergence between the two is most likely to reflect sampling error
in the HLFS.
• Meanwhile, the employer-focused Quarterly Employment Survey (QES) recorded a 0.1% rise
in the number of filled jobs and a 0.6% rise in hours paid. (This survey is of course vulnerable
to sampling error as well.)
• We’re most inclined towards the MEI measure of employment, given the breadth of its

coverage. But our best interpretation is that, to the extent that jobs are growing, it’s not
yet fast enough to absorb the growth in the adult population – hence the further rise in the
unemployment rate.

### Page 4

Westpac New Zealand Economics
4
Labour force participation
• The surprising fall in HLFS employment was accompanied by a drop in the participation rate
from 70.6% to 70.4%.
• These co-movements are effectively a reversal of the surprisingly large increases that we
saw in the June quarter, which we noted at the time were suggestive of sampling error in the
survey. Any such errors largely wash out when it comes to the unemployment rate, however,
which is why we recommend focusing on this measure.

• Setting aside the usual quarterly volatility, labour force participation remains caught
between two competing forces. One is cyclical weakness, especially among younger people,
who are finding it difficult to break into a “low-fire, low-hire” labour market. The teen
unemployment rate of around 25% is a record high going back to the start of the survey
in 1986.
• The other force is rising participation among the over-60s (who also make up a growing

share of the population). Kiwis have had a growing propensity to work beyond the NZ Super
age, as there is no penalty for doing so. It’s unclear to what extent this can be considered
an increase in the pool of available workers: older workers tend to have low mobility
between jobs, and if they find themselves out of work are more likely to leave the labour
force altogether.
